I don't really know. I guess... I mean, I'd still see it myself, so... probably? But I'm not sure. I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be less concerned about the appearance of your face if everyone around you assured you that it looks fine?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if that would help. I mean... I still have to see it myself. When I'm editing those lesson videos, it's just... right there. I can't stop seeing how crooked and swollen it is. I just... I guess I just think about it. I don't really know if they... pop in, exactly. I just... I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about your face cause you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. It's... it's hard to deal with. I don't really know how to describe it, but... yeah, it bothers me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried that your face looks ugly in general, or is it specifically that one side looks crooked and swollen?\n\nPatient: Um... it's... it's specifically the one side. It just... it looks crooked, and kind of swollen. I... I have to record these lesson videos for school, and... I just can't stop seeing it when I watch them back. Once I notice it, it's just... I feel like that's all anyone else is looking at, too. Like the students, or the parents... or the other staff. Like they're just staring at my face instead of listening to what I'm teaching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face actually looks crooked and swollen, or do you just worry that others will think it does?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I mean... I see it. So I guess I think it actually is. But... I don't really know how to separate the two. I just... I know what I see. I'm not really sure how else to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be bothered by the appearance of your face if you didn't have to interact with others, like your students and colleagues?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I guess... I mean, I'd still see it myself, so... probably? But I'm not sure. I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be less concerned about the appearance of your face if everyone around you assured you that it looks fine?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if that would help. I mean... I still have to see it myself. When I'm editing those lesson videos, it's just... right there. I can't stop seeing how crooked and swollen it is. So even if people said it was fine, I'd still know it's there. And I'd still feel like... like the students and the parents and the other teachers are just... staring at it instead of listening to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would be possible for you to change your appearance to fix the crooked and swollen side of your face, or do you think it's something that cannot be changed?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I haven't really looked into surgery or anything like that. I just... I don't know if it could be fixed. I try not to think about it that way.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about the appearance of your face decrease if you were alone and didn't have to present yourself to others?\n\nPatient: Um... I think you asked me something like that a minute ago. I... I really don't know. Like I said, I'd still see it myself, so... I'd probably still be thinking about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to fix the appearance of your face if you were the only person who knew about it?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. Like I said before, it's just... I'd still know it's there. So... I guess I still wouldn't like it. I'm not really sure.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your face go away if you could be certain that no one else noticed or cared about its appearance?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like you keep asking me the same thing. Um... no. Even if no one else cared, it's still my face. I'd still have to look at it in the mirror and see how messed up it is. So... no, I don't think it would just go away. I really don't know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your face decrease if you were completely alone and had no possibility of interacting with others?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know why you keep asking me this. I really don't have a different answer for you. Like I just said... no. Being alone wouldn't change anything.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your face more about how it looks to you or about what others might think of it?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. It's... it's really just my face. I don't... no, nothing else.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about your face pop into your mind when you don't want them to?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I just... I guess I just think about it. I don't really know if they... pop in, exactly. I just... I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about your face cause you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. It's... it's hard to deal with. I don't really know how to describe it, but... yeah, it bothers me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried that your face looks ugly in general, or is it specifically that one side looks crooked and swollen?\n\nPatient: Um... it's... it's specifically the one side. It just... it looks crooked, and kind of swollen. I... I have to record these lesson videos for school, and... I just can't stop seeing it when I watch them back. Once I notice it, it's just... I feel like that's all anyone else is looking at, too. Like the students, or the parents... or the other staff. Like they're just staring at my face instead of listening to what I'm teaching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face actually looks crooked and swollen, or do you just worry that others will think it does?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I mean... I see it. So I guess I think it actually is. But... I don't really know how to separate the two. I just... I know what I see. I'm not really sure how else to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be bothered by the appearance of your face if you didn't have to interact with others, like your students and colleagues?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I guess... I mean, I'd still see it myself, so... probably? But I'm not sure. I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be less concerned about the appearance of your face if everyone around you assured you that it looks fine?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if that would help. I mean... I still have to see it myself. When I'm editing those lesson videos, it's just... right there. I can't stop seeing how crooked and swollen it is. So even if people said it was fine, I'd still know it's there. And I'd still feel like... like the students and the parents and the other teachers are just... staring at it instead of listening to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would be possible for you to change your appearance to fix the crooked and swollen side of your face, or do you think it's something that cannot be changed?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I haven't really looked into surgery or anything like that. I just... I don't know if it could be fixed. I try not to think about it that way.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about the appearance of your face decrease if you were alone and didn't have to present yourself to others?\n\nPatient: Um... I think you asked me something like that a minute ago. I... I really don't know. Like I said, I'd still see it myself, so... I'd probably still be thinking about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to fix the appearance of your face if you were the only person who knew about it?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. Like I said before, it's just... I'd still know it's there. So... I guess I still wouldn't like it. I'm not really sure.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your face go away if you could be certain that no one else noticed or cared about its appearance?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like you keep asking me the same thing. Um... no. Even if no one else cared, it's still my face. I'd still have to look at it in the mirror and see how messed up it is. So... no, I don't think it would just go away. I really don't know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your face decrease if you were completely alone and had no possibility of interacting with others?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know why you keep asking me this. I really don't have a different answer for you. Like I just said... no. Being alone wouldn't change anything.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your face more about how it looks to you or about what others might think of it?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. Both, maybe? I just... I don't know how to separate it like that. It just bothers me. I don't really have a better answer for you.\n\nDoctor: Is it the fact that you see the flaw in your face that bothers you the most, or is it the thought of others noticing it?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like we're just going in circles. I don't... I don't know which one is worse. They both just feel bad. I can't really break it down like that.\n\nDoctor: Is it the fact that you see the flaw in your face that bothers you the most?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know what else to say. I thought I just answered this. I just see it and it bothers me. I can't really give you a different answer.\n\nDoctor: Is it the fact that you believe your face is actually flawed that bothers you, or is it more the fear of what others might think when they see it?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know why we keep going over this. You keep asking the exact same thing, and I... I don't have a different answer for you. It's just bad. All of it. I don't know what else you want me to say.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I'm here because... I guess I've been having some trouble at work lately. I'm a teacher, and... well, we have to record our lesson videos now. \n\nAnd... when I watch them back, I just... I can't stop looking at my face. It's... the side of it, it looks all crooked and... swollen. And once I see it, I just... I can't think about anything else. I feel like... like my students, and their parents, and the other staff... they must all be staring at it, too. Like they're not even listening to what I'm teaching.\n\nDoctor: Is it only your face that you're concerned about, or are there other parts of your body that you worry about as well?\n\nPatient: Um... no. It's... it's really just my face. I don't... no, nothing else.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about your face pop into your mind when you don't want them to?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I just... I guess I just think about it. I don't really know if they... pop in, exactly. I just... I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about your face cause you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. It's... it's hard to deal with. I don't really know how to describe it, but... yeah, it bothers me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried that your face looks ugly in general, or is it specifically that one side looks crooked and swollen?\n\nPatient: Um... it's... it's specifically the one side. It just... it looks crooked, and kind of swollen. I... I have to record these lesson videos for school, and... I just can't stop seeing it when I watch them back. Once I notice it, it's just... I feel like that's all anyone else is looking at, too. Like the students, or the parents... or the other staff. Like they're just staring at my face instead of listening to what I'm teaching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face actually looks crooked and swollen, or do you just worry that others will think it does?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I mean... I see it. So I guess I think it actually is. But... I don't really know how to separate the two. I just... I know what I see. I'm not really sure how else to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be bothered by the appearance of your face if you didn't have to interact with others, like your students and colleagues?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I guess... I mean, I'd still see it myself, so... probably? But I'm not sure. I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be less concerned about the appearance of your face if everyone around you assured you that it looks fine?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if that would help. I mean... I still have to see it myself. When I'm editing those lesson videos, it's just... right there. I can't stop seeing how crooked and swollen it is. So even if people said it was fine, I'd still know it's there. And I'd still feel like... like the students and the parents and the other teachers are just... staring at it instead of listening to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would be possible for you to change your appearance to fix the crooked and swollen side of your face, or do you think it's something that cannot be changed?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I haven't really looked into surgery or anything like that. I just... I don't know if it could be fixed. I try not to think about it that way.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about the appearance of your face decrease if you were alone and didn't have to present yourself to others?\n\nPatient: Um... I think you asked me something like that a minute ago. I... I really don't know. Like I said, I'd still see it myself, so... I'd probably still be thinking about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to fix the appearance of your face if you were the only person who knew about it?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. Like I said before, it's just... I'd still know it's there. So... I guess I still wouldn't like it. I'm not really sure.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your face go away if you could be certain that no one else noticed or cared about its appearance?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like you keep asking me the same thing. Um... no. Even if no one else cared, it's still my face. I'd still have to look at it in the mirror and see how messed up it is. So... no, I don't think it would just go away. I really don't know what else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Would your concern about your face decrease if you were completely alone and had no possibility of interacting with others?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know why you keep asking me this. I really don't have a different answer for you. Like I just said... no. Being alone wouldn't change anything.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your face more about how it looks to you or about what others might think of it?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. Both, maybe? I just... I don't know how to separate it like that. It just bothers me. I don't really have a better answer for you.\n\nDoctor: Is it the fact that you see the flaw in your face that bothers you the most, or is it the thought of others noticing it?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like we're just going in circles. I don't... I don't know which one is worse. They both just feel bad. I can't really break it down like that.\n\nDoctor: Is it the fact that you see the flaw in your face that bothers you the most?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know what else to say. I thought I just answered this. I just see it and it bothers me. I can't really give you a different answer.\n\nDoctor: Is it the fact that you believe your face is actually flawed that bothers you, or is it more the fear of what others might think when they see it?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know why we keep going over this. You keep asking the exact same thing, and I... I don't have a different answer for you. It's just bad. All of it. I don't know what else you want me to say.\n\nDoctor: Is it the fact that you believe your face is actually flawed that bothers you?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know how else to say it. Yes. It bothers me. I just... I don't know what kind of answer you're looking for at this point. I really don't have anything else to add.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.     "psychosocial_stressor": null,
    "additional_requirements": ["preoccupation with appearance is not better explained by concerns with body fat or weight"]
  }
}
